The Sozialgesetzbuch (SGB) (Social Code) is subdivided into twelve books that are described by a Roman numeral (number thirteen was omitted, the last book is number fourteen). For instance, Sozialgesetzbuch V Gesetzliche Krankenversicherung deals with social health insurance.


Statutes and decrees
Citation format: <statute or decree full name or, if available and part of the statutory title, short title> [<abbreviated statute or decree name, if available>] [<English translation of short title>], <date of enactment>, <name of gazette of promulgation> [<abbreviation of gazette of promulgation>] at <volume, page, and/or sentence number of gazette, if desired>, <“as amended,” or amending law with citation, if applicable>, <relevant subdivisions, if desired> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).
Finanzmarktstabilisierungsfonds-Verordnung [FMStFV] [Financial Markets Stabilization Regulation], Oct. 20, 2008, Elektronischer Bundesanzeiger [eBAnz] at 123 2008 VI (Ger.).


Subsequent citation may be made to the abbreviation of the gazette, rather than the full name of the publication.



Waffengesetz [WaffG] [Weapons Act], Oct. 11, 2002, BGBl. I at 3970, § 1 (Ger.).


Bundes-Bodenschutz-und Altlastenverordnung [BBodSchV] [Federal Soil Protection and Contaminated Sites Ordinance], July 12, 1999, BGBl I at 1554, § 2, no. 6 (Ger.).


Einfuhrliste [List of Import Restrictions], Dec. 15, 2003, BAnz at 26, 137 (Beilage no. 243a) (Ger.).


Statutes are often amended by subsequent legislation. In these cases, citing the original enactment does not indicate what amendments have since occurred. Add “as amended,” or cite a particular amending law. Add a translation of the statute name if helpful and cite an English translation of the statute if available, or provide a link to the consolidated online version, if available.



Aktiengesetz [AktG] [Stock Corporation Act], Sep. 6, 1965, BGBl I at 1089, last amended by Gesetz [G], July 17, 2017, BGBl I at 2446, art. 9 (Ger.), https://www.gesetze-im-interne....


If statutes are revised to provide a completely updated text after their initial enactment, cite the first enactment, followed by the date and citation of the revised version (Neufassung).



Bundesnotarordnung [Federal Act on Notaries Public], Feb. 13, 1937, RGBl II at 191, revised Feb. 24, 1961, BGBl I at 97 (Ger.).


Cite a Gesetz (G) (federal statute) or Verordnung (V) (federal regulation) to the federal law gazette where it was promulgated, either the BGBl or the RGBl, or, in the case of some regulations, BAnz or eBAnz. When the BGBl or RGBl is divided into Teil I (part I) and Teil II (part II), domestic legislation can be found in the first volume.



Bundesgesetzblatt, Teil I	1951–date	BGBl I
Bundesgesetzblatt, Teil II 	1951–date	BGBl II
Reichsgesetzblatt
1871–1921	RGBl
Reichsgesetzblatt, Teil I 	1922–1945	RGBl I
Reichsgesetzblatt, Teil II 	1922–1945	RGBl II
Bundesanzeiger
1949–date	e.g., BAnz at <page number> (Beilage no. <number>)
Elektronischer Bundesanzeiger	2004–date	e.g., eBAnz at <document number>
Bundessteuerblatt Teil I	1951–date	BStBl I
Bundessteuerblatt Teil II	1951–date	BStBl II
Legislative materials
There are two federal legislative bodies: the Deutscher Bundestag (BT) (Federal Parliament) and the Deutscher Bundesrat (BR) (Federal Council). Cite published Drucksachen (literally "publications," i.e., legislative materials) by legislative period and number for BT and by number and last two digits of year for BR (optionally add link to online version).
Regierungsentwurf [Cabinet Draft], Deutscher Bundestag: Drucksachen[BT]  17/13423, http://dip21.bundestag.de/dip2... /17/134/1713423.pdf (Ger.).


Regierungsentwurf [Cabinet Draft], Deutscher Bundesrat: Drucksachen [BR] 257/06 (Ger.).

Treaties and conventions
Cite treaties and conventions to BGBl II, and use the citation format for statutes.
Internet sources
Free websites
Up-to-date consolidated versions of German laws are available on many websites, some published by German agencies and offering reliable up-to-date versions of numerous laws.


Gesetze im Internet, http://www.gesetze-im-internet.de, published by the Federal Ministry of Justice in partnership with juris, provides the up-to-date text of most laws and regulations.


Gesetz über Urheberrecht und verwandte Schutzrechte [Urheberrechtsgesetz] [UrhG] [Copyright Act], Sep. 9, 1965, BGBl I at 1273 (Ger.), http://www.gesetze-im-internet....


Statutes provided on this site are usually updated within one month. Unless indicated by the text, a citation of the amended status is not typically necessary. For some statutes, translations are also available.


Other resources
https://www.bundesanzeiger.de/ebanzwww/wexsservlet (regulations published in the electronic Bundesanzeiger (eBAnz))
The federal courts have websites that make their decisions available:

http://www.bundesverfassungsgericht.de (Bundesverfassungsgericht – Federal Constitutional Court)
https://www.bundesgerichtshof.de (Bundesgerichtshof – Federal Constitutional Court)
http://www.bvger.de (Bundesverwaltungsgericht – Federal Administrative Court)
https://www.bundesfinanzhof.de (Bundesfinanzhof – Federal Fiscal Court)
https://www.bundesarbeitsgericht.de (Bundesarbeitsgericht – Federal Labor Court)
https://www.bsg.bund.de (Bundessozialgericht – Federal Social Court)
Federal legislative materials:

http://dip.bundestag.de (Bundestag)
http://www.parlamentsspiegel.de (Bundesrat)

Commercial databases
Germany’s comprehensive legal databases juris and BeckOnline are available by subscription:

https://www.juris.de
https://beck-online.beck.de


Commentaries: The most important secondary source for academics and practitioners are commentaries (comparable to treatises in the U.S.). Beside the suggestion by the editors for how to cite the respective commentary (usually found at the very beginning of the commentary), a widely-accepted general way of citing is: <author (multiple: author/author)> in: <title of commentary>, <volume (if necessary), edition/year of publishing/status of editing>, <section and recital(s)> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).



Andreas Spickhoff in: MüKoBGB, 8th ed. 2018, § 1 recital 1 (Ger.).


Ulrich Schallmoser in: Blümich EstG, July 2019, § 16 recitals 667–702 (Ger.).

T2.14.1 – German Länder
Cases
In the integrated German court system, the trial and appellate courts are state courts, and the courts of last resort are federal courts. These courts adjudicate matters of federal and state law.
Constitutional jurisdiction
Staatsgerichtshof (StGH) or Verfassungsgerichtshof (e.g., BayVerfGH for Bavaria) (constitutional court that adjudicates matters relating to the state’s constitution): Cite to the official reporter, a periodical, or a website.



BayVerfGH July 19, 2007, 60 Sammlung von Entscheidungen des Bayerischen Verwaltungsgerichtshofs mit Entscheidungen des Bayerischen Verfassungsgerichtshofs [BayVGH (NF)] 151 (Bavaria) (Ger.).

Constitutions
Constitutions (Verfassung) of the German states (Länder) are designated as such, followed by the name of the state in question:
Verfassung Bayern	Verf Bay
Statutes and decrees
Cite a Landesgesetz (LG) (state statute) or Landesverordnung (LVO) (state administrative regulation) to the law gazette (GVBl.) of the individual state.
Bayerisches Verfassungsschutzgesetz, repromulgated Apr. 10, 1997, Bayerisches Gesetz- und Verordnungsblatt [Bay GVBl] 70 (Bavaria) (Ger.).


Bayerisches Gesetz- und Verordnungsblatt	Bay GVBl
Internet sources
Länder laws and regulations, as well as decisions of the Länder courts, are available on their official websites. For instance, in the Land of North Rhine-Westphalia, the Ministry of Justice provides a legal library that contains the court decisions of North Rhine-Westphalia as well as the most important laws of the Federation and the other Länder. The portal is available in English at http://www.justiz.nrw.de/WebPo..., but the sources are in German. For additional electronic sources, see table T2.14 Germany, Federal Republic of.]

## T2.16 Hong Kong

[T2.16 Hong Kong
(Common Law)
Chinese and English language sources
Hong Kong has two official languages: English and Chinese. Where a source is available in both languages, generally cite to the English source, unless there is a specific reason to cite to the Chinese version. When citing an English language primary source, it is not necessary to preface the source with the words “translated in.”


Romanization
For romanization of Chinese language citations, follow instructions in rule 20.2.4(b).


English abbreviations
Follow abbreviation guidelines for U.S. materials, using tables T6 and T10, unless an exception is provided in table T2.43 United Kingdom. Render “Hong Kong Special Administrative Region” or “HKSAR” as “Hong Kong.”


Cases
Citation format: <case name>, [<year of decision>] <volume number or part> <reporter abbreviation> <first page>, <page(s) of specific material, if desired> (<court abbreviation>) (<jurisdiction abbreviation if not evident from context>).
Law Soc’y of H.K. v. Brook, [1997–1998] 1 H.K.C.F.A.R. 228, 231D (C.F.I.).


Hong Kong v. Chan Hing Hung, [1998] 4 H.K.C. 487, 488C (C.F.I.).


Tong Ping-chuen v. AG, [1990] 1 H.K.L.R. 551, 559E (H.C.).

District Court (D.C.): For an online source, cite using application number, abbreviation, and date as provided in the judgment. Cite to D.C.L.R. or Legal Reference System, if therein. Provide a parallel print source citation where available.

## T2.18 India

[T2.18 India
(Common Law)
India is a constitutional republic with a federal structure. India’s Parliament is bicameral: consisting of the House of the People (Lok Sabha) and the Council of States (Rajya Sabha). Indian states have their own legislative assemblies. India has a union judiciary, consisting of the Supreme Court and various High Courts.



English is the primary language of the legal system of India. Publication of cases, laws, regulations, and reporting systems are all in English.


Cases
null Supreme Court of India (SCI) (Federal Court (India Fed.) from 1937 to 1950).
 Supreme Court of India (SCI) (Federal Court (India Fed.) from 1937 to 1950): Cite to the Supreme Court Reports (SCR).
Citation format: <case name>, [<year of reporter>] <volume number> SCR <first page> [<page(s) of specific material, or paragraph number(s) of specific material, if desired>].



Common Cause v. Union of India, [2018] 6 SCR 1.


Note, there are several authorized and unauthorized reporters for the Supreme Court, state high courts, district courts, tribunals, and industry specific reporters. The tables below are a non-exhaustive list of reporters and the citation formats of other reporters may depart from the standard format, as illustrated below.



Common Cause v. Union of India, (2018) 5 SCC 1.


Navtej Singh Johar v. Union of India, AIR 2018 SC 4321.


Supreme Court Reports	1950–date	[<year>] <volume> SCR <page>.
All India Reporter	1914–date	AIR <year> SC <page>.
Supreme Court Cases	1969–date	 (<year>) <volume> SCC <page>.
Supreme Court Almanac	1979–date	<year> (<volume>) SCALE <page>.
Federal Court Reports	1939–1950	<year> F.C.R. <page>.
Indian Cases	1909–1947	<volume> Ind. Cas. <page>.
Criminal Law Journal	1904–date	(<year>) <volume> Cri LJ <page>.
Accidents Claims Journal	1966–date	<year> (<volume>) A.C.J. <page>.
Indian Factories & Labor Reports	1960–date	<year> (<volume>) F.L.R. <page>.
Labor & Industrial Cases	1968–date	(<year>) <volume> L.L.J  <page> (SC).
Labor Law Journal	1957–date	(<year>) <volume> L.L.J <page> (SC).
Sales Tax Cases	1970–date	 (<year>) <volume> S.T.C. <page> (SC).
SCC Online (online only)	1991–date	<year> SCC Online SC <page>.
Manupatra (online only)	backdated
catalog	MANU/SC/<page>/<year>.

Recent judgments of the Supreme Court have a neutral citation, a format independent of the publishing source that allows for immediate online publishing.



Citation format: <case name>, <year> <neutral citation abbreviation> <case reference number>, [<paragraph(s) of specific material, if desired>] (<jurisdiction abbreviation if not evident from context>).



Union of India v. Barakathullah, 2024 INSC 452, [3] (Supreme Court of India).


For unreported judgments, the citation format is as follows:

<case name>, <case type> No. <case number> of <year>, <page/paragraph> (decided <date>) (Supreme Court of India).



High Courts (e.g., Madras HC) (highest state courts (Supreme Courts (e.g., Calcutta SC) prior to 1860)) and Courts of the Judicial Commissioner (e.g., CJC Manipur) (Sadar Dewani Adalats (e.g., SDA Agra) prior to 1860)): Cite to AIR, SCC, SCCOnline, or Manupatra but append the name of the court.



Alimentary Health Limited v. Controller of Patents and Designs, 2024 SCC OnLine Del 3473 (High Court of Delhi).


Tribunals: Cite to a widely used reporter, such as Company Cases for the National Company Law Tribunal (NCLT).



Citation format: <case name>, <citation as provided in the reporter>, [<page(s) of specific material, or paragraph number(s) of specific material, if desired>] (<name of Tribunal>, <name of bench if any>).



Ajit Bhagwan Kulkarni v. State Bank of India, 23 Company Cases 444, 447 (NCLAT, Principal Bench).


District Courts: Cite to judgments available on ecourts.gov.in in the following format:

<case name>, <case type> No. <case number> of <year>, <page/paragraph> (decided <date>) (<name of court>).



If a judgment is not available on eCourts but is available online from an unofficial source, cite to it using rule 18.



Rahul Gandhi v. Purnesh Modi, Criminal Appeal No. 254 of 2013 (April 20, 2023) (Court of the 8th Additional Sessions Judge at Surat).

Constitution
In citing the Constitution and its amendments the following form may be used:
India Const. art. 19, cl. 1(a).


India Const. art. 269, amended by The Constitution (Eightieth Amendment) Act, 2000.

Statutes
For statutes, cite to the version published in the Gazette of India.
Citation format: <short statute title>, <year>, § <section number> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).


Ordinances
For ordinances, include the date of the ordinance as well.



The National Food Security Ordinance, 2013, § 3 (July 5, 2013).

Internet Sources
For citations to an internet source, use rule 18, and preferably cite to indiacode.nic.in. Legislation made available on https://indiacode.nic.in is not regularly updated. Reference should be made to legislation available on legal databases such as Manupatra and SCCOnline. If possible, verify versions available against the Gazette.


Rules and Regulations
A government publication is preferred over other sources. The gazette published by the Government of India or state government, as applicable, must be the primary source of citation.



Citation format: <rule/regulation name>, <year of rule/regulation>, Rule/Reg. <rule/regulation number> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).



Transplantation of Human Organs and Tissues Rules 2014, rule 5.


Securities and Exchange Board of India (Index Providers) Regulations 2024, reg. 4.

Administrative Directions
These include circulars, directions, guidelines, and government orders. A government publication is preferred over other sources. The gazette published by the Government of India or state government, as applicable, must be the primary source of citation.



Citation format: <name of issuing authority>, <notification name>, <notification number, if available> (<indicate whether it is the date on which it was issued or the date on which it was notified> on <date of notification/issuance>)(<jurisdiction abbreviation if not evident from context>).



Ministry of Finance, S.O. 387(E) (notified on January 27, 2020).


Ministry of Corporate Affairs, CAB Order, F.No. 52/26/Cab-2010 (issued on June 30, 2011).

Parliamentary Materials
Bills
Citation format: <bill name>, <year>, Bill No. <bill number> of <year>, cl. <clause number> <explanatory notes> para. <paragraph number(s)> (<indicate the version of the bill used>) (<jurisdiction abbreviation, if not evident from context>).



The Telecommunications Bill 2024, Bill No. 194-F of 2023, cl. 1
(as passed by Lok Sabha) (India).


Debates
Cite the official report of the proceedings of the Lok Sabha and the Rajya Sabha, which is published their official secretariats.



Citation format: <series> <volume number in roman numerals> <number> Lok Sabha Debates <page> (<date>) (<jurisdiction, if not evident from context>).



17 XXIX 9 Lok Sabha Debates 33 (10 February 2024).


When the official debates are not available, cite using rule 18, and prefer the uncorrected verbatim debates on the sansad.in website.


Reports and Other Documents
Citations to reports of parliamentary committees should include the name, session, and other details of the parliament committee.



Citation format: <government department or committee name>, <report title>, <report number, if any>, <session year>, <abbreviation of applicable House of Parliament where report was issued> <paper number>, <paragraph symbol and number, or “at” page number of specific material> (<jurisdiction abbreviation if not evident from context>).



Public Accounts Committee (2023-24), Manpower and Logistics Management in Delhi Police (140th Report, 17th Lok Sabha).


Citations to reports of expert commissions or committees set up by the government should be cited using rule 15.


Internet sources
The latest status of court cases that are either pending or disposed of, and judgments and orders issued by the Supreme Court or any state High Court can be found at https://main.sci.gov.in/case-status.
Judgment Information System of the Supreme Court of India provides free access to all judgments of the Supreme Court and certain High Courts, as well as the SCR journal from 1950 to present at https://main.sci.gov.in/judgments.



The text of primary and delegated legislation pending or passed by the Parliament, state legislatures, and central and state governments can be found online. The Gazette of India and gazettes for some states can be assessed online at http://egazette.nic.in. Several states also have separate websites for the e-gazettes.



Parliamentary bills, including texts, and other enactments may also be searched and located on the website of the Government of India at https://mpa.gov.in.



Legislative documents, including statutes, bills, legislative history, etc. can also be found at the website of PRS Legislative Research at https://prsindia.org.]

## T2.19 Iran, Islamic Republic of

[T2.19 Iran, Islamic Republic of
(Civil/Islamic Law)
Cases
Iran has a three-tiered court system, but since the 1979 Revolution, lower-court cases have not been reported, and the only Supreme Court cases reported have been unification cases (cases taken to resolve split decisions by different branches of the Supreme Court or by lower courts). Such cases from 1944 to 2002 are contained in a one-volume reporter issued by the Law Research Directorate of the President's Office in 2003. It organizes cases chronologically by subject. The title of this volume is as follows:
Majmū‘ah-i ārā’-i Vahd. at-i Ravīyah-i Dīvān-i ‘ālī-i Kishvar [Compilation of the Unification Decisions of the Supreme Court].


Citation format: <serial number> <court name> [<English translation of the court name>] <case number>–<date on Iranian calendar in month/day/year format> [<date on Gregorian calendar in month/day/year format>] <first page>, <page(s) of specific material, if desired> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).



17 Marja‘-i Rasīdagī bih›Amr-i Tāba‘īyat [Court Having Jurisdiction to Hear a Citizenship Case] 701–01/20/1381 [1/20/2003] 1372 (Iran).


Historically, following the dissolution of the Islamic religious courts, introduction of the European legal system, and formation of what was then called ‘Adlīyah-i Jadīd (New Justice) in Iran in 1927, it was not the practice to report court decisions. In 1954, the decisions of the then-eight chambers of the Supreme Court, as well as the decisions made at the general meetings (the court sitting en banc) of the Supreme Court from 1932 to 1954, were published. This serial collection continued its publication annually, with a change of publisher and format in 1964, until the 1979 Revolution.



Majmū‘ah-i Ravīyah-i Qaz. ā’ī [Compilation of Jurisprudence].


Court of Administrative Justice: Article 173 of the 1979 Constitution established the Court of Administrative Justice in order to investigate complaints and grievances concerning civil servants, organs, or state agencies and regulations. Currently, the Court has ten chambers. The Official Gazette of the Islamic Republic of Iran regularly publishes the decisions of the Court. The Court also provides a database of all its authoritative decisions.



Citation format: <date on Iranian calendar in year/month/day format> [<date on Gregorian calendar>], File No. <file number>, <case name> [<English translation of the case name>] (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).



1382/9/23 [Dec. 9, 2004], File No. 446/79, Raye Heyati Umoomii Divani Idaalati Idari [Ruling of the General Meeting of the Administrative Justice Tribunal] (Iran).

Constitutions
Iran has had two constitutions, as indicated below. Cite by the name of the constitution or amendment, followed by an English translation in brackets, and the year of the constitution or amendment (either in the Iranian calendar with the Gregorian equivalent in brackets, or in the Gregorian calendar without brackets). To refer to a particular passage, include the article number.
Qanuni Assassi Jumhurii Islamai Iran [The Constitution of the Islamic Republic of Iran] 1358 [1980].


Islahat Va Taqyyrati Va Tatmimah Qanuni Assassi [Amendment to the Constitution] 1368 [1989] (Iran).


Qanuni Assaassi Iran [Iranian Constitution] 1906.


According to the Constitution of 1980, the Guardian Council ensures that the House of Representatives passes no law inconsistent with Islamic law or the constitution. The decisions of the Guardian Council are published under the following title:



Majmuahi Nazariati Shurai Nigahban [Compilation of the Opinions of the Guardian Council] Tehran 1992.

Codes
Citation format: <name of the code> [<English translation of the name of the code>] [<abbreviation for the code, if applicable>] <year of publication> [<if the year has been given in the Iranian calendar, then append the date from the Gregorian calendar in brackets>], <article number(s)> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).
Qanuni Madani [Civil Code] 1314 [1935], arts. 656–83 (Iran).


Aini Dadrassii Madani [Civil Procedure Code] 1379 [2000] (Iran).


Majmuahi Kamili Qavanini Va Muqarrati Bazergani Va Tijari [Code of the Commercial Laws and Regulations] 1370 [1990] (Iran).


Majmuahi Qavanini Jazai [Code of Criminal Laws] 1381 [2002] (Iran).


Majmuahi Qavanini Niruhai Mussalahi Jumhurii Islamii Iran [Compilation of the Laws of the Armed Forces of the Islamic Republic of Iran] 1364 [1985].


Majmuahi Qavanini Sabti [Land Registration Laws] 1382 [2003] (Iran).

Statutes and regulations
Citation format: <statute or regulation name> of <date of enactment in day month year format> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>). Or: <type of law> of <date of enactment in day month year format> (<subject of law>) (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).
State Monetary and Banking Law of 9 July 1972, amended 1975 (Iran).


Law of 12/13 May 1982 (Nationalization of Foreign Trade) (Iran).


Act of 19 Feb. 1988 (Direct Taxation) (Iran).


Regulations of 2 June 1984 (Establishment, Operation, and Dissolution of Representative Offices of Foreign Banks) (Iran).

Official Gazette
The Official Gazette is published daily. Its supplement is nominally published on a weekly basis, but in reality publications are irregular. The Iranian Ministry of Justice publishes these publications and the annual compilation.
Citation format for an Iranian gazette: Ruznamehi Rasmi Jumhuri Islami Iran [The Official Gazette of the Islamic Republic of Iran], <statute or regulation citation>, <serial number> <first page>, <page(s) of specific material, if desired> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).



Or: Zamimahi Ruznamehi Rasmi [Supplement to the Official Gazette of the Islamic Republic of Iran], <statute or regulation citation>, <serial number> <first page>, <page(s) of specific material, if desired> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).



Or: Majmuahi Qavanin Salaneh [Annual Compilation of Laws], <statute or regulation citation>, <first page>, <page(s) of specific material, if desired> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).



Citation for the 1945–1979 Official Gazette: Ruznamehi Rasmi Kishvari Shahanshahi [The Imperial Iranian Official Gazette], <statute or regulation citation>, <serial number> <first page>, <page(s) of specific material, if desired> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).


Internet sources
http://www.rrk.ir/Default.aspx (Ruznamahi Rasmi (Iranian Official Journal)))
https://guides.loc.gov/law-iran (Library of Congress Guide to Law Online: Iran)]

## T2.20 Iraq, Republic of

[T2.20 Iraq, Republic of
(Civil Law)
Romanization
Use the ALA-LC RomanizationTables: Transliteration Schemes for Non-Roman Scripts, approved by the Library of Congress and the American Library Association, available at http://www.loc.gov/catdir/cpso/romanization/arabic.pdf.

Cases
Citation format: <name of court> [<short name of court>] [<name of court and/or chamber in English>] [<short name of court in English>], <region if applicable>, <decision number, if available> of <date of decision, spelled out>, <reporter or journal> [<name of reporter or journal in English>], <volume of journal, if applicable>, <year of publication>, <first page> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).
Mah. kamat al-Tamyīz [Tamyīz] [Court of Cassation] [Cassation], deci- sion No. 1428 of March 6, 1974, Al-Nashrah al-Qadā’īyah [Judicial Report], vol. 1, year 5, p. 161 (Iraq).

Courts
Mah. kamat al-Tamyīz (Tamyīz) (Court of Cassation: highest court of ordinary jurisdiction, sits only in the capital and has several chambers). Citation is sufficiently recognized by referring to this court as “Mah. kamat al-Tamyīz” or simply “Tamyīz.”



Tamyīz [Cassation, civil chamber], decision No. 1509 of March 18, 1974, al-Nashrah al-Qadā’īyah, vol. 1, year 5, p. 11 (Iraq).


Mah. kamat al-Isti’nāf (Isti’nāf) (Court of Appeal: general court of second instance). Cite with the name followed by the geographic area.



Isti’nāf Baghdād [Court of Appeal, Baghdad], decision No. 48 of September 1985, Majmū‘at al-Ahkām al-‘Adlīyah [Compilation of Judicial Decisions], issues 3 and 4 of 1985, p. 141 (Iraq).

Sources
Cite to the following publications of the Iraqi Government:



al-Nashrah al-Qaḍā’īyah

Tamyīz [Cassation], decision No. 901 of 1973, al-Nashrah al-Qaḍā’īyah, vol. 1, year 5, p. 247 (Iraq).


Majmūʻat al-Aḥkām al-ʻAdlīyah

Istiʼnāf Baghdad [Court of Appeal, Baghdad], decision No. 1872 of 1979, Majmūʻat al-Aḥkām al-ʻAdlīyah, vol. 4, year 5, p. 112 (Iraq).

Statutes, codes, and decrees
Citation format: <name and number of the law> of <year> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>). Or: <subject of the law> Law of <year> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>). Or: <name or subject of the law> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).
Residency of Foreigners Law No. 118 of 1987 (Iraq).


Weapons Law of 1992 (Iraq).


Civil Procedures Law (Iraq).

Official Gazette
The official gazette of Iraq is called al-Waqā’i‘ al-‘Irāqīyah and is published periodically by the Iraqi government. Cite by the issue number and date of publication.



Citation format: <title of the law>, al-Waqā’i‘ al-‘Irāqīyah [Iraqi Official Gazette] <issue number> of <date of issue> <page number, if desired> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).


Constitutions
Iraq has had several constitutions since its creation after World War I. If referring to a particular provision, provide the article and section number before the name.
Article 12, al-Qānūn al-Asāsī al-‘Irāqī [The Organic Law] of 1924 (Iraq).


al-Dustūr al-‘Irāqī al-Mu’aqqat [The Interim Iraqi Constitution] of 1958.


al-Dustūr al-‘Irāqī al-Mu’aqqat [The Interim Iraqi Constitution] of 1964.


al-Dustūr al-‘Irāqī al-Mu’aqqat [The Interim Iraqi Constitution] of 1968.


al-Dustūr al-‘Irāqī al-Mu’aqqat [The Interim Iraqi Constitution] of 1970.


Article 4, Section 2, Dustūr Jumh. ūrīyat al-‘Irāq [The Constitution of the Republic of Iraq] of 2005.

Internet sources
http://ar.parliament.iq (Iraqi Parliament website with a number of laws and proposed draft laws) http://www.moj.gov.iq/tmeez (Iraqi Ministry of Justice website; includes limited number of Court of Cassation decisions) http://www.moj.gov.iq/facts (Iraqi official gazette)]

## T2.21 Ireland (Eire), Republic of

[T2.21 Ireland (Éire), Republic of
(Common Law)
Cases
Citation format: <case name> [<year>] <volume number, if one exists> <neutral citations, if applicable> <reporter/publication abbreviation, if available, and unreported cases> <first page of case, or opinion number in that calendar year for neutral citations and unreported cases> (<court abbreviation, unless citing to the highest court or indicated by neutral citation> <year of decision if not already provided>) (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).
Ryan v. Att’y Gen. [1965] IR 294 (Ir.).


KSK Enters. Ltd. v. An Bord Pleanála [1994] 2 IR 128, 135 (Ir.).


DPP v. Carolan [1998] 2 ILRM 212 (H. Ct.) (Ir.).


Gilligan v. Special Criminal Court [2005] IESC 86 [2006] 2 IR 406 (Ir.).


The Irish Legal Information Initiative, https://irlii.org/, can help to determine whether a neutral citation number has been issued for a case.



Supreme Court of Éire (SC), High Court (H. Ct.), and Court of Criminal Appeal (CCA): Cite to IR, if therein; otherwise, cite to one of the reporters below:



Employment Law Reports 	1990–date	ELR
Irish Company Law Reports 	1963–1993	ICLR
Irish Reports	1868–date	IR
Irish Law Times Reports  	1866–1980	ILTR
Irish Law Reports Monthly	1976–date	ILRM

Irish Jurist Reports  	1935–1966
1849–1866	Ir. Jur. Rep.
Irish Tax Reports  	1922–1927	ITR
Frewen  	1924–1989	Frewen
Constitution
Citation format: Constitution of Ireland 1937 art. <article number>. Or: Constitution of the Irish Free State 1922 art. <article number>.
Constitution of Ireland 1937 art. 2, http://www.irishstatutebook.
ie/eli/cons/en/html.
Constitution of the Irish Free State 1922 art. 5.

Constitution of Ireland (Bunreacht na hèireann)	Constitution of Ireland 1937.
Constitution of the Irish Free State (Saorstát Éireann) Act 1922	Constitution of the Irish Free State 1922.
Statutory compilations and session laws
Citation format: <short title, including year if in title> (<Act No., or if before 1938, Pub. Stat. No.>/<year>) (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).
Online references should be to the Irish Statute Book, where available.



Control of Exports Act 2008 (Act No. 1/2008) (Ir.), http://www.irishstatutebook.ie/eli/2008/act/1/enacted/en/html.


Dublin Transport Authority Act 2008 (Act No. 15/2008) (Ir.), http://www.irishstatutebook.ie/eli/2008/act/15/enacted/en/html.


Acts of the Oireachtas
1937–date
Acts of the Oireachtas (Saorstát Éireann)	1922–1937
Administrative materials
Citation format: <short name of instrument or order> (SI <number>/<year>) (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>). Or: <short name of instrument or order> (SR & O <number> of <year>) (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).
Online references should be to the Irish Statute Book, where available.



Control of Fishing for Salmon Order 2008 (SI 98/2008) (Ir.), http://www.irishstatutebook.ie/eli/2008/si/98/made/en/print.


Irish Nationality and Citizenship Regulations 2005 (SI 1/2005) (Ir.), http://www.irishstatutebook.ie/eli/2005/si/1/made/en/print.


Eggs (Regulation of Export to Germany) Order 1936 (SR & O 89 of 1936) (Ir.), Redirected to: http://www.irishstatutebook.ie/eli/1936 /sro/89/made/en/print.


Statutory Instruments	1947–date	SI
Statutory Rules and Orders	1922–1947	SR & O
Parliamentary debates
Citation format: <volume number> <publication abbreviation> (<date>) col. <column number> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).
83 Dáil Deb. (Feb. 17, 1979) col. 556 (Ir.).


87 Seanad Deb. (Mar. 21, 1977) col. 970 (Ir.).


Parliamentary Debates, Dáil Debates	1922–date	Dáil Deb.
Parliamentary Debates, Seanad Debates	1922–date	Seanad Deb.

Citation format for debates that occurred prior to the availability of the print version: <volume and number as given by the online source> <printed publication abbreviation>, <section of the debate being cited> (<date>) (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>), <URL>.



212 No. 9 Seanad Deb., Business of Seanad (Jan. 11, 2012) (Ir.).

Committee or commission reports
Citation format: <author name>, <title> (<Parliament number if available>, <year of publication if available>) (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).
Gov’t of Ir., Inter-Departmental Working Group on Abortion, Green Paper on Abortion (1999).

Citation format: <treaty name>, ITS No. <treaty number>/<year> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).

## T2.22 Israel

[T2.22 Israel
(Mixed Jurisdiction)
Cases
In general, citations to Israeli court cases must denote the case type. For Case type abbreviations, see Case Type Abbreviations Table below. If the relevant case type is not found in in the Appendix, use the word “Case” instead.
Supreme Court

Israel’s highest court functions both as the country’s Supreme Court of Appeals, hearing appeals from lower courts, and as the High Court of Justice (HCJ), hearing constitutional and certain administrative petitions. Cite to Piske Din reporter (PD), sometimes referred to as IsrSC.



Citation format: <case type abbreviation> <case number> <party A> v. <party B>, <volume number> (<part number>) <reporter abbreviation> <first page>, <page(s) of specific material, if desired> (<Gregorian calendar year of decision>) (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).



CivA 1482/92 Hager v. Hager, 47(2) PD 793 (1993) (Isr.).


HCJ 852/86 Aloni v. Minister of Justice, 41(2) PD 1, 5 (1987) (Isr.).


District Courts (DC)

These courts operate in six judicial districts–Jerusalem (Jer), Tel-Aviv-Jaffa (TA), Haifa (Hi), Be’er-Sheva (BS), Nazareth (Nz), and Central District (CT), hearing appeals from Magistrate Courts, and serving as court of first instance, usually in certain administrative, civil, and criminal matters. Some district courts also serve as special courts, e.g., DC TA and DC Hi also serve as DC for Economic Affairs, and DC Jer also serves as the Competition Law Court. Cite to applicable reporter, such as Psakim Mehoziim (PM).



Citation format: <case type abbreviation> (DC <court location>) <case number> <party A> v. <party B>, <reporter abbreviation> <Hebrew calendar year of volume> (<part number>) <first page>, <page(s) of specific material, if desired> (<Gregorian calendar year of decision>) (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).



CrimC (DC TA) 521/91 Ben Moshe Kokhavi v. State of Israel, PM 5753(2) 267 (1993) (Isr.).


Magistrate Courts (MC)

These courts operate in twenty-eight cities and serve as the courts of first instance in most civil and criminal cases. Cite to applicable reporter, such as Psakim Mehoziim (PM). If court location is other than one of the District Courts’ locations, do not abbreviate and use full name of location.



Citation format: <case type abbreviation> (MC <court location>) <case number> <party A> v. <party B>, <reporter abbreviation> <Hebrew calendar year of volume> (<part number>) <first page>, <page(s) of specific material, if desired> (<Gregorian calendar year of decision>) (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).



Religious Courts

For recognized communities, religious courts generally have jurisdiction in matters of marriage and divorce. Some decisions of the Rabbinical Courts are periodically published in Piske din shel batei ha-din ha-rabaniyim be-Yisrael (PDR). To distinguish between first-instance cases and cases on appeal, note the location of the first-instance courts in order to be consistent with citation of the decisions of the District Courts.



Citation format: Case (<court name> <court location, if applicable>) <case number> <party A> v. <party B>, <reporter abbreviation> <volume number> <first page>, <page(s) of specific material, if desired> (<Gregorian calendar year of decision, if available>) (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).



Case (Rabbinical Court TA) 103/717 A. v. B., PDR 2 65 (1956) (Isr.).


Other Courts

If the case cited is from a different tribunal, such as local or national Labor Court or Military tribunals, follow the District Courts citation format and indicate the full name of the tribunal.



Electronic databases

Many reported and unreported decisions are available on widely used electronic databases, such as the Nevo Legal Database at http://www.nevo.co.il or the Takdin Legal Database at http://www.takdin.co.il (both by subscription, in Hebrew). For Supreme Court cases, do not mention court name and court location.



Citation format: <case type abbreviation> (<court name> <court location>) <case number> <party A> v. <party B>, <page(s) of specific material, if desired> <database name> (<date of decision>) (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>), <URL, if available>.



CivC (DC TA) 42549-03-16 Ben Menahem v. Weiss, Nevo Legal Database (Nov. 1, 2018) (Isr.), https://www.nevo.co.il/psika_html /mechozi/ME-16-03-42549-33.htm.


Case (Military Court of Appeals Judea & Samaria) 4987/08 Hasin Nafa v. Military Prosecution, Nevo Legal Database (Nov. 13, 2008) (Isr.).

Case Type Abbreviations Table
Administrative Appeal	AdminA
Administrative Case	AdminC
Bankruptcy Case (individuals)	BankC
Civil Appeal	CivA
Civil Case	CivC
Criminal Appeal	CrimA
Criminal Case	CrimC
Derivative Case	DerivC
Family Appeal	FamA
Family Case	FamC
High Court of Justice	HCJ
Leave to Administrative Appeal	LAdminA
Leave to Civil Appeal	LCivA
Leave to Criminal Appeal	LCrimA
Leave to Family Appeal	LFamA
Leave to Small Claim Appeal	LSClaimA
Liquidation Case (corporations)	LiqC
Request for Leave to Appeal	ReqLA
Severe Criminal Case	SCrimC
Small Claim	SClaim
Statutes
The Knesset of Israel serves as the parliament and legislates all primary legislation, including the Basic Laws (a string of laws of constitutional statute). Cite to Laws of the State of Israel (LSI), which contains the authorized English translation of legislative enactments from 1949 to 1989. Citation to LSI is useful for English readers for laws enacted prior to 1989 and for the original text of legislation that has since been amended.
Citation format: <section, if desired>, <statute name>, <Hebrew calendar year of enactment (if appended to statute name)>–<Gregorian calendar year of enactment (if appended to statute name)>, <compilation abbreviation> <volume number> <first page> (<coverage date range of volume>), <as amended (if relevant)> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>), <URL, if available>.



§ 2, Extradition Law, 5714–1954, LSI 8 144 (1953–54), as amended (Isr.).


If citation to the LSI is impossible (for example, if the translation is partial or is not available), cite to the applicable official reporter, including Sefer HaHukim (SH) (“The Laws Book,” the main reporter); Dinei Medinat Israel (DMI) (“State of Israel Laws”); Hu’kei Eretz Israel (HEI) (“Statutes of the Land of Israel”); I’ton Rishmi Mandatori (IRM) (“Official Bulletin of the Mandate Government”); I’ton Rishmi–Mo’etzet HaMedina HaZmanit (IRMMZ) (“Official Bulletin–the Provisional State Council”).



Citation format: <section, if desired>, <statute name>, <Hebrew calendar year (if appended to statute name)>–<Gregorian calendar year (if appended to statute name)>, <reporter abbreviation> <issue number> (<Gregorian calendar year of reporter, if not appended to statute name, or if different than statute year>) <first page>, <page(s) of specific material, if desired> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>), <URL, if available>.



§2, Protection of Witnesses Law, 5769–2008, SH 2192 98, 99 (Isr.), http://www.nevo.co.il/Law_word/law14/LAW-2192.pdf.


Basic Law: the Knesset, SH 244 (1958) 69 (Isr.).


A shorter citation format can be used when a citation is made to the updated version of the statute (as opposed to a citation of the original version):



Citation format: <section, if desired>, <statute name>, <Hebrew calendar year (if appended to statute name)>–<Gregorian calendar year (if appended to statute name)> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>), <URL, if available>.



Basic Law: the Judiciary (Isr.).


Penal Law, 5737–1977.

Legislative history
For draft bills from 1949 to 2002 cite to Hatza’ot Hok (HH). Starting in 2003, for draft bills submitted by the Government cite to HH (Gov.); for those submitted by the Knesset (Israel’s parliament) cite to HH (Knesset). Private member bills are only available on the Knesset website, at https://main .knesset.gov.il/Activity/Legislation/Laws/Pages/LawSuggestionsSearch .aspx?st=allsuggestions&ki=-1&ls=54. Identify such bills as private member bills and cite to the Knesset website.
Citation format: <section, if desired>, <proposed statute name>, <Hebrew calendar year (if appended to statute name)>–<Gregorian calendar year (if appended to statute name)>, <reporter abbreviation (with identification of general, government, Knesset, or private member bill, as appropriate)> <issue number> <first page>, <page(s) of specific material, if desired> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>), <URL, if available>.



Draft Bill Basic Law: Human Dignity and Liberty, HH 2086 60 (Isr.), https://www.nevo.co.il/law_word/Law17/PROP-2086.pdf.


Draft Bill for Replacement of the Term Employer (Legislative Amend- ments), 5774–2014, HH (Knesset) 535 44 (Isr.), https://www.nevo.co .il/law_word/Law16/knesset-535.pdf.


Draft Bill for the Prescription Law, 5764–2004, HH (Gov.) 125 611 (Isr.), https://www.nevo.co.il/law_html/Law15/MEMSHALA-125.pdf.


Draft Bill for Service by Electronic Mail, 5777-2017 (Private Member Bill) (Isr.), https://fs.knesset.gov.il//20/law/20_lst_381859.docx


For protocols of Knesset proceedings, cite to Divrei HaKnesset (DK) from 1949 to date; available online, at https://m.knesset.gov.il/Activity/plenum/ Pages/Sessions.aspx.



Citation format: DK, <Knesset term>, Knesset, Session No. <session number>, (<Gregorian calendar year of proceedings>) <page(s) of specific material, if desired> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>), <URL, if available>.



DK, 20th Knesset, Session No. 56 (2015) (Isr.), https://fs.knesset.gov.il//20/Plenum/20_ptm_315141.doc.


DK, 6th Knesset, Session No. 129 (1966) (Isr.), https://fs.knesset.gov.il/6/Plenum/6_ptm_252678.pdf.

Secondary legislation
If allowed by the primary legislation of the Knesset, designated government ministers or other governmental departments and commissioners may enact secondary legislation (such as regulations, municipal bylaws, and other rules) under the relevant statute. Cite secondary legislation materials to the appropriate reporter, including Kovetz HaTakanot (KT) (“Collection of Regulations,” which contains regulations of the State of Israel from 1949 to date); Kovetz HaTakanot–Hi’kukei Shilton Mekomi (KTHSM); and Yalkut HaPirsumim (YP).
Citation format: <section, if desired>, <secondary legislation name>, <Hebrew calendar year (if appended to statute name)>–<Gregorian calendar year (if appended to statute name)>, <reporter abbreviation> <issue number> <first page>, <page(s) of specific material, if desired> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>), <URL, if available>.



Knesset Elections Regulations (Instructions Regarding Voting Proce- dures by Soldiers, Employees, and Persons under Military Custody), 5773–2012, KT 7196 362 (Isr.).


Bylaw for Tel Aviv-Yafo (Street Paving), 5763–2003, KTHSM 663 166 (Isr.), https://www.nevo.co.il/law_word/Law07/MEKOMI-0663.pdf.

Guidelines by governmental agencies
Attorney General and State Attorney
Citation format: <title of guidelines>, <guidelines number> <name of agency> Guidelines, <page number, if desired> (<date>), <URL, if available>.



Handling Matters Concerning Unauthorized Propaganda, 1.1903 Attorney General Guidelines (updated Jul. 2003), https://www.justice.gov.il/Units/ YoezMespati/HanchayotNew/Seven/11903.pdf.


Other Governmental Agencies
Citation format: <number of guidelines/instructions> <type of guidelines/ instructions>, <title> (<full date>), <URL, if available>.



301 Proper Bank Management Guidelines, The Board of Directors (Nov. 13, 2018), https://www.boi.org.il/he/BankingSupervision /SupervisorsDirectives/DocLib/301.pdf.

Orders by the Military, Police, Prison Services, and National Fire and Rescue Authority
Citation format: <order number> <type>, <title> <URL, if available>.



21.0113 General Staff of the IDF Order, Limitations on Use of Personal Phones in the IDF, https://www.idf.il/media/16624/210113.pdf.

Treaties
Cite to Kitvei Amana (KA), which contains treaties and conventions signed by Israel.
Citation format: <treaty name>, <volume number> <reporter abbreviation> <issue number>, <first page>, <page(s) of specific material, if desired> (<Gregorian calendar year of publication>) (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>), <URL, if available>.



Convention for the Suppression of the Financing of Terrorism, 1401 KA 50 1 (2004) (Isr.), https://www.nevo.co.il/law_html/Law09/AMANA- 1401.pdf.

Secondary sources
Law review articles Citation format: <author(s) full name>, <title of article>, <issue number> <journal name> (<volume number, if appropriate>) <first page>, <page(s) of specific material, if desired> (<Gregorian calendar year of publication>), <URL, if available>.
Alon Klement and Daphna Kapeliuk, Procedural Contracts, 33 Iyuney Mishpat 187 (2010).


Hebrew law (Mishpat Ivri)

When citing to the Hebrew Bible, use the full name of the book. If the book name includes a number, place the number before the name. Chapters and verses are denominated using Hebrew letters; therefore, they should be translated to numbers.



Citation format: <book number, if appropriate> <name of book> <chapter number>, <verse number>.



Exodus 20, 12.


1 Samuel 1, 12.


When citing to the Talmud, use the full form name, i.e., Mishnah, Babylonian Talmud (Talmud Babvli) or Jerusalem Talmud (Talmud Yerushalmi). Page numbers are denominated by Hebrew letters, which should be translated into numbers, and each page has two sides (“a” and “b”).



Citation format: <name of source>, <tractate name>, <verse/page/halakha number>.



Babylonian Talmud, Megillah 15a.


When citing to the Mishnah, cite to the appropriate chapter and paragraph (mishnah) after translating the Hebrew letters into numbers.



Citation format: <name of source>, <tractate name> <chapter number>:<paragraph/mishnah>.



Mishnah Berakhot 1:3.

Internet sources
http://www.knesset.gov.il (The Knesset (Israeli parliament); available in English) https://www.gov.il/en/Departments/the_judicial_authority (Judicial Authority; available in English) https://supreme.court.gov.il/sites/en/Pages/home.aspx (Supreme Court; available in English) https://www.gov.il/en/departments/prime_ministers_office (The Prime Minister’s Office; available in English) https://www.gov.il/en/departments/ministry_of_foreign_affairs (Ministry of Foreign Affairs; available in English) https://www.gov.il/en/Departments/the_rabbinical_courts (Rabbinical Courts; available in English) http://nevo.co.il (Nevo Legal Database; by subscription, in Hebrew)
http://takdin.co.il (Takdin Legal Database; by subscription, in Hebrew) https://www.loc.gov/law/help/guide/nations/israel.php (Library of Congress, Guide to Law Online: State of Israel) https://www.llrx.com/2001/01/features-a-guide-to-the-israeli-legal-system (guide to the Israeli legal system by Ruth Levush via LLRX) https://www.gov.il/en/subjects/law_and_government (main government portal)]

## T2.23 Italy, Republic of

[T2.23 Italy, Republic of
(Civil Law)
Cases
Constitutional Court
Citation format: <court name> [<English translation of court name>], <date of decision>, <decision number>, <optionally, URL of online publication on the Constitutional Court’s website> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).



Corte Cost. [Constitutional Court], 5 December 2023, n. 225 (It.).


Supreme Court of Cassation



Citation format: <abbreviation of court name> <either section of the court in Roman numbers or united sections>, <type of court (either civil, shortened as “civ.” for civile, or criminal, shortened as “pen.” for penale)> [<English translation of court name>], <date of decision>, <decision number>, <review or other location where the decision is published> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).



Cass. Civ., Sez. II [Court of Cassation], 28 March 2024, n. 8440 (It.).


Cass. Civ., Sez. Un. [Court of Cassation], 19 March 2024, n. 7337 (It.).


State Council



Citation format: <court name> [<English translation of court name>], <number of the section issuing the decision in Roman numbers>, <clarify whether the act is not a decision>, <date of decision>, <decision number>, <optionally, URL of online publication on the State Council’s website> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).



Decision:



Cons. Stato [Council of State], Sez. VII, 10 November 2023, n. 11395 (It.).


Action other than a decision:



Cons. Stato [Council of State], Sez. VII, ordinance,15 December 2023, n. 11396 (It.).


Other Courts



For other courts, including intermediary appellate (Corte d’Apello, shortened as App.) and first-instance courts (Tribunale, shortened as Trib.), use the following format, to the extent applicable: <court name> [<English translation of court name>], <date of decision>, <decision number>, <review or other location where the decision is published> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).



For Italian courts and their English translations, consult the following table:

Corte d’Assise d’Appello	Assize Court of Appeal

Constitution
The Constitution of the Italian Republic came into effect on January 1, 1948. An English translation of the Constitution can be found on this page: https://www.senato.it/istituzione/la-costituzione.
Costituzione Italiana [Constitution of the Italian Republic], Art. 1 (It.)

Laws, Executive Decrees, and Decisions
Citation format: <article, paragraph>, <name of law or decree> [<English translation of law or decree>], <number of law or decree, if available>, <date of law or decree, if available> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).
Art. 1321 Cod. Civ. [Italian Civil Code] (It.)


Art. 4, Decreto Legislativo [Legislative Decree], n. 385, 1 September 1993 (It.).

Codice di procedura penale	c.p.p. or Cod. Pen. Pro.	Criminal Procedure Code

## T2.24 Japan

[T2.24 Japan
(Civil Law)
Romanization
For romanization, word division, and capitalization of Japanese words, apply the rules of the American Library Association and the Library of Congress: Use the modified Hepburn romanization system adopted by Japanese-English dictionaries, such as Kenkyūsha shin Wa-Ei daijiten (Kenkyūsha’s New Japanese-English Dictionary). Indicate long vowels with a macron as in “hōrei” or “hanreishū.” Add an apostrophe between ‘n’ and a vowel, and ‘n’ and ‘y’asin“gen’in,”or“kin’yū,”toclarifythemeaningofwords.Fordetails,seethe Library of Congress ALA-LC Romanization Tables at http://www.loc.gov/ catdir/cpso/roman.html.

## T2.26 Lebanon, Republic of

[T2.26 Lebanon, Republic of
(Civil Law)
Romanization
Use the ALA-LC Romanization Tables: Transliteration Schemes for Non-Roman Scripts, approved by the Library of Congress and the American Library Association, available at http://www.loc.gov/catdir/cpso/romanization/arabic.pdf.


Cases
Citation format: <court name> [<court abbreviation>] [<English translation of court name>], <region if applicable>, <number and/or date of decision in day month year format>, <reporter or journal name> [<reporter or journal abbreviation>], <section of journal if applicable>, <year of publication>, p. <first page> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).
Mah. kamat al-Tamyīz [Tamyīz] [Court of Cassation], 10 Dec. 1962, Majmū‘at Bāz [Bāz], 1962, p. 73 (Leb.).


Courts

Lebanon has different categories of courts, including religious courts dealing mainly with family law matters. The most commonly cited courts are as follows:



Ma h. kamat al-Tamyīz (Tamyīz) (Court of Cassation: highest court of ordinary jurisdiction). This court sits only in the capital and has several chambers referred to generally by numbers (Chamber 1, Chamber 2, and so forth). Citation is sufficiently recognized by referring to this court only as “Tamyīz” or “Qarār (decision) Tamyīz.”



Mah. kamat al-Isti’nāf (Isti’nāf) (courts of appeals: general courts of second instance). Lebanon is divided into a number of appellate jurisdictions. Citation is sufficiently recognized by referring to this court as “Isti’nāf” or “Qarār Isti’nāf,” followed by the name of its geographic jurisdiction.



Mah. kamat al-Darajat al-Ūlā (Courts of First Instance). This court is composed of two sections, the section of the “Qād. ī Munfarid” ( Judge of First Instance), and the section of the “Ghurfat Mah. kamat al-Darajat al-Ūlā” (Chamber of First Instance). Lebanon is divided into a large number of first instance jurisdictions. Decisions of first instance are seldom published or cited. Citations are sufficiently recognized by referring to the section (judge or chamber of first instance) or to the “h. ukm” (judgment) of such sections followed by the name of its geographic jurisdiction.


Sources for cases
Case citations can be made to the following publications:


al-Nashrah al-Qad. ā’īyah (al-Nashrah), an official publication of the Lebanese Ministry of Justice containing full texts of court decisions.



Majmū‘at Bāz (Bāz), a private publication issued on a yearly basis containing the legal reasoning of the Court of Cassation decisions.



Majmū‘at H. ātim (H. ātim), a private publication identified by its consecutive issue numbers containing selected portions of court decisions.



Majallat al-‘Adl (‘Adl or al-‘Adl), an official publication of the Beirut Bar. It is published quarterly and has a section for court cases.


Statutes and decrees
Citation format: <statute or decree name or number> of <date in day month year format> (<translation of subject matter of the law, if desired>) (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).
Legislative Decree 138 of 7 Sep. 1983 (Leb.).


Decree-Law 144 of 12 June 1959 (Taxation on Natural and Juridical Persons) (Leb.).


Decree 2,385 of the French High Commissioner of 17 Jan. 1924 (Leb.).


Law of 17 Sep. 1962 (Taxation on Structures Built on Land) (Leb.).


Two compilations of Lebanese legislation currently in force (in Arabic), published in Beirut are:


Majmū‘at al-Tashrī’ al-Lubnānī
Qawānīn Lubnān: Majmū‘at al-nus.us. al-Tashrī‘īyah wa-al-Tanz. īmīyah
Official Gazette
The Official Gazette, al-Jarīdah al-Rasmīyah, is published weekly and divided into two parts. The first contains laws, decree-laws, and decrees and decisions by ministries.The second part contains official notices and announcements.
Internet sources
http://www.lp.gov.lb (site of the Lebanese Parliament where the constitution and electoral laws are posted) http://www.loc.gov/law/help/guide/nations/lebanon.php (Library of Congress Guide to Law Online: Lebanon) http://www.law.cornell.edu/world/mideast.html#lebanon (online guide to legal materials and other information about Lebanon) http://www.pcm.gov.lb/arabic/index.aspx?pageid=5 (website of the Presidency of the Council of Ministers where issues of the official gazette since 2006 are posted)]

## T2.27 Malaysia

[T2.27 Malaysia
(Civil/Islamic Law)
Malaysia operates two legal systems simultaneously—civil law and Syariah law. Civil laws are based on the common law tradition and are applicable to all persons. In addition, Syariah law covers personal and family matters applicable to Muslims.


Cases
Citation format: <case name, in italics> <year of publication in parentheses> <volume number> <reporter abbreviation> <first page>, [<paragraph(s) of specific material, if desired>] (<court name, if necessary>).
Civil cases: The most commonly-cited reporters in Malaysia are the Malayan Law Journal (MLJ) and the Current Law Journal (CLJ). Both MLJ and CLJ provide reports of cases from the Federal Court, the Court of Appeal and High Courts.



Cubic Electronics Sdn Bhd (In Liquidation) v. Mars Telecommunications Sdn Bhd [2019] 6 MLJ 15, [30] (Federal Court).


Syariah cases: CLJ provides reports of Syariah Court decisions in Malaysia starting from 2004. The reporter is abbreviated as “CLJ(Sya)” prior to 2014 and as “SHR” 2014 onwards.



Ismail Yahya v Ketua Pendakwa Syarie Terengganu [2015| 1 SHR 97.

Constitution
Malaysia’s Federal Constitution should be cited as follows:
Fed. Const., art. 8(5)(a) (Malay.).

Statutes
Malaysian statutes consist of federal Acts and state Enactments. Civil laws are enacted in federal Acts, whereas Syariah laws are enacted in state Enactments. State laws enacted in the state of Sarawak are known as Ordinances. Current federal Acts and relevant subsidiary legislation are available online at https://lom.agc.gov.my.
Federal Acts citation format: <act name> <year of passage> (<act number>), <section(s), if applicable> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).



Land Public Transport Act 2010 (Act 715), § 16 (Malay.).


State Enactments will usually contain the name of the enacting state in the enactment name. In the first example below, the enacting state is Selangor. If the name of the state is not in the enactment name, place it in front as per the second example below:

(<state name, if applicable>) <enactment name> <year of passage> (<enactment number>), <section(s), if applicable>.



Muslim Wills (Selangor) Enactment 1999 (En. 4/1999), § 17(1).


(Sarawak) Syariah Courts Ordinance 2001 (Cap. 42/2001), § 22(1).

Subsidiary Legislation
Subsidiary legislation is published in the Federal and State Gazettes respectively:
Land Public Transport (Terminal Licensing) (Station and Transport Hub) Regulations 2024 (P.U. (A) 42/2024) (Malay.).


(Sarawak) Housing Development (Control and Licensing) Regulations 2014 (Swk. L.N. 105/2014).

Bills
Citation format: <bill name> <year of introduction in Parliament> <bill code>, <clause(s), if applicable>.
Jurisdictional Immunities of Foreign States Bill 2023 (D.R.41/2023), cl. 4.

Parliamentary Debates
The Malaysian Parliament is a bicameral system which consists of the Dewan Rakyat (House of Representatives) and the Dewan Negara (Senate). The parliamentary debates (Hansard) for both assemblies are only published in the Malay language, at https://www.parlimen.gov.my.
Citation format: <name of assembly> Hansard <date of Hansard> <number of Hansard> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).



House of Representatives Hansard 5 Mar. 2024 (No. 6/2024) (Malay.).]

## T2.28 Mexico

[T2.28 Mexico
(Civil Law)
Cases
Mexico does not have an official system of citation for cases decided by courts at the federal level. It is customary to cite federal cases provided in the Court Reporter's digital system in the following format:
Citation format for cases decided by the Mexican Supreme Court and federal courts: <rubro>, <court name> [<court abbreviation>] <reporter name> <Época>, Tomo <volume number> <month and year of reporter> <number of decision in reporter> <case number> página <first page of decision> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).



Personalidad. En contra de la resolución que dirime esta cuestión, previamente al fondo, procede el amparo indirecto, Pleno de la Suprema Corte de Justicia [SCJN], Semanario Judicial de la Federación y su Gaceta, Novena Época, Tomo XIII, Enero de 2001, Tesis P./J. 4/2001, página 11 (Mex.).


The "rubro," or "case title" in English, is a short statement prepared by a court on the topic and issue discussed in the case.  "Épocas" or "time periods" in English, are periods of time determined by the Supreme Court of Mexico in order to organize cases by chronological order.  As of 2021, the Supreme Court was in the eleventh época.  The first four épocas are no longer in force.  The last seven épocas (from the fifth through the eleventh) include cases that are currently in force.  All cases decided by federal courts indicate the época in which the case was decided.


Federal court structure
The Suprema Corte de Justicia de la Nación (SCJN) is the highest court of constitutional and federal jurisdiction in Mexico and sits as a full court (pleno) or in chambers. The Tribunales Colegiados de Circuito (TCC) are the equivalents, to some extent, of the U.S. federal courts of appeals.



Juzgado de Distrito (District Courts)	JD
Plenos de Circuito (Full Circuit Courts)	PC
Suprema Corte de Justicia de la Nación (Supreme Court of Justice of the Nation)	SCJN
Tribunal Agrarios (Agrarian Courts)	TA
Tribunal Colegiado (Three-Judge Appellate Court)	TC
Tribunales Colegiados de Circuito (Three-Judge Circuit Courts)	TCC
Tribunal Electoral del Poder Judicial de la Federación (Electoral Court of the Judiciary of the Federation)	TEPJF
Tribunal Federal de Conciliación y Arbitraje (Federal Court of Conciliation and Arbitration)	TFCA
Tribunal Federal Electoral (Federal Electoral Court)	TFE
Tribunal Federal de Justicia Fiscal y Administrativa (Federal Court of Tax and Administrative Justice)	TFJFA
Tribunal Superior Agrario (Superior Agrarian Court)	TSA
Tribunales Unitarios Agrarios (One-Judge Agrarian Courts)	TUA
Tribunales Unitarios de Circuito (One-Judge Circuit Courts)	TUC
 Case law publications
Cases decided by federal courts in Mexico are currently published in the Semanario Judicial de la Federación y su Gaceta (SJFG) (Federal Judicial Weekly Report and its Gazzette). Only cases decided by SCJN, PC, and the TCC are published in the SJFG. The SJFG volumes are published monthly.



Anales de Jurisprudencia (Annals of Jurisprudence)	AJ
Apéndice al Semanario Judicial de la Federación (Appendix to the Federal Judicial Weekly Report)	ASJF
Boletín Judicial (Judicial Bulletin)	BJ
Boletín de Información Judicial (Bulletin of Judicial Information)	BIJ
Boletín del Semanario Judicial de la Federación (Bulletin of the Federal Judicial Weekly Report)	BSJF
Informe de la Suprema Corte de Justicia (Report of the Supreme Court of Justice)	Info.
Jurisprudencia por Contradicción de Tesis (Jurisprudence by Contradiction)	JCT
Juzgados de Distrito (District Courts)	JD
Precedentes Relevantes (Relevant Precedents)	PR
Semanario Judicial de la Federación (Federal Judicial Weekly Report)	SJF
Semanario Judicial de la Federación y su Gaceta (Federal Judicial Weekly Report and Its Gazette); or Gaceta del Semanario Judicial de la Federación (Gazette of the Federal Judicial Weekly Report)	SJFG or GSJF
Tribunal Federal Electoral (Federal Electoral Court)	TFE
Tribunal Fiscal de la Federación (Tax Court of the Federation)	TFF
Tribunal Federal de Justicia Fiscal y Administrativa (Federal Court of Tax and Administrative Justice)	TFJFyA
Constitution
Constitución Política de los Estados Unidos Mexicanos	CP or CPEUM

Citation format: <Constitution Name> [<constitution abbreviation>], <specific provision(s) cited, if applicable>, Diario Oficial de la Federación [DOF] <date of original publication>, <date of latest revision, if applicable> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).



Constitución Política de los Estados Unidos Mexicanos, CP, Diario Oficial de la Federación [DOF] 05-02-1917, últimas reformas DOF 10-02-2014.


Constitución Política de los Estados Unidos Mexicanos, CPEUM, Diario Oficial de la Federación [DOF] 05-02-1917, últimas reformas DOF 10-02-2014.

Codes
Citation format: <code name> [<code abbreviation>], <specific provision(s) cited, if applicable>, Diario Oficial de la Federación [DOF] <original date of publication>, <date of latest revision, if applicable>  (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).
Código Civil [CC], Diario Oficial de la Federación [DOF] 14-05-1928, últimas reformas DOF 24-12-2013 (Mex.).


Código Civil [CC], art. 214, Diario Oficial de la Federación [DOF] 14-05-1928, últimas reformas DOF 24-12-2013 (Mex.).


Código Civil Federal (Federal Civil Code)	CC

The Mexican government publishes legislative and executive materials in the Diario Oficial de la Federación (DOF), which is the official gazette of the federal government in Mexico.  The DOF provides ready access to the official text of federal laws, presidential documents, administrative regulations and notices, treaties, and descriptions of federal organizations, programs, and activities. Some administrative orders issued by the Supreme Court are published in the DOF as well. Cite to DOF for all federal legislation.

## T2.29 Netherlands, Kingdom of the

[T2.29 Netherlands, Kingdom of the
(Civil Law)
Cases
Citation format: <court abbreviation> <city (except when citing HR)> <date of decision in Dutch>, <reporter abbreviation> <publication year>, <publication number> m.nt. <initials or name of annotator> (<plaintiff name>/<defendant name>) (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).
HR 30 januari 1959, NJ 1959, 548 m.nt. DJV (Quint/Te Poel) (Neth.).


Hof ’s-Gravenhage 27 mei 2004, JOR 2004, 206 m.nt. Van Ravels en Van Andel (Vie d’Or/Stichting Pensioen- en Verzekeringskamer en Staat) (Neth.).


Until June 28, 2013, many court decisions published in the Netherlands received a National Reports Number (LJN), consisting of two letters and four digits. After that date, decisions are to have a European Case Law Identifier (ECLI), a standard that was adopted in late 2010. For purposes of conversion into ECLI coding, the LJN is included in the final (numeric) part of ECLI, e.g., ECLI:NL:GHAMS:2014:566, where NL is for Netherlands and GHAMS is Gerechtshof Amsterdam. A search for cases on websites such as Rechtspraak.nl, https://uitspraken.rechtspraak.nl/#!/, can be done using the identifier.


Ordinary jurisdiction
Hoge Raad der Nederlanden (HR) (Supreme Court of the Netherlands), Gerechtshof (Hof) (ordinary courts of appeals), Hoog Militair Gerechtshof (HMG) (Military Court of Appeals) (abolished in 1991), and Krijgsraad (Kr.) (Court Martial) (no longer in existence): Cite to NJ or W, if therein.



Prior to 2002, there were two courts of first instance: Arrondissementsrechtbank (Rb.) (ordinary court of first instance and court of appeals to the Kantongerecht) and Kantongerecht (Ktg.) (court of first instance for labor, rent, and misdemeanor cases). In 2002, these two courts merged, eliminating the latter court as a separate court (instead, it is now a subdivision of the former court). Currently, labor, rent, and small claims (rather than misdemeanor) cases are still decided by a different kind of judge—the Kantonrechter (Ktr.)—than are other civil cases. Because of the merger, the Arrondissementsrechtbank (Rb.) is no longer a court of appeals to the Kantongerecht (Ktg.). After the merger, those specific types of cases should be cited to the Kantonrechter (Ktr.) instead of the Kantongerecht (Ktg.), following the form mentioned above. Cite to Prg. or NJF, if therein.

The Algemene Wet Bestuursrecht (Awb.) (General Administrative Law Act), enacted in 1992, is not technically a code, but it provides a general foundational framework for Dutch administrative law.


Statutes and decrees
Citation format: <law or decree> <date>, <gazette abbreviation> <year>, <page number> (<country abbreviation if not evident from context>).

The Staatsblad van het Koninkrijk der Nederlanden (Stb., sometimes cited as S.) is the official gazette of the Kingdom of the Netherlands. It is the official source for finding and citing the authoritative text of all codes, laws, general administrative measures, and royal decrees.



The Nederlandse Staatscourant (Stcrt.) is an official daily journal used for the publication of some royal decrees, ministerial decrees, ministerial circulars, and other official statements.



A parallel citation to Nedersandse Staatswetten (Schuurman & Jordens) or Nederlandse Wetgeving (Cremers) may be given.

Verdrag inzake de heffing van rechten voor het gebruik van bepaalde wegen door zware vrachtwagens; Brussel, 9 februari 1994 Trb. 2014, 126.
